What brings you in?\n\nPatient: I guess what finally pushed me to come in is my eating’s been really weird lately. Like, some days I’ll just completely forget to eat, and then other nights I’m up after midnight just tearing through takeout and snacks. I know that sounds kind of small, but it freaked me out a bit. My work schedule’s always been a little chaotic too—sales stuff, driving around, grabbing food whenever—but this feels more all over the place than usual.\n\nDoctor: When those late-night eating episodes happen, do you feel unable to stop or control how much you’re eating?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sort of. Yeah… I don’t know if I’d call it “depressed” exactly, but I just don’t care about stuff like I used to. Like weekends, I used to play soccer, and I’d actually look forward to it. But lately it feels more out of control, I guess.\n\nDoctor: When those late-night episodes happen, do you eat what most people would consider a very large amount of food?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. I mean, I’m not sitting there measuring it or anything, but it’ll be like a whole takeout order and then chips or whatever snacks are around after midnight. And then the weird part is the next day I might realize I barely ate at all, because I was driving around for work and just forgot. That’s kind of what scared me—how all over the place it’s gotten.\n\nDoctor: After those nights when you eat a lot, do you ever make yourself vomit?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve felt gross after, like too full and annoyed with myself, but I haven’t made myself throw up. I usually just kind of sit there and feel stupid about it, or try to sleep, and then the next day I’m distracted with work stuff again.\n\nDoctor: After those nights when you eat a lot, do you try to make up for it by exercising hard, fasting, or using laxatives?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know… I mean, I haven’t, like, tracked it that way. Sometimes the next day I might not eat much, but that could just be because I’m driving all over for work and I get busy, not because I’m trying to punish myself or something. I don’t do the whole gym thing to make up for it—honestly my schedule’s too weird for that. And laxatives, no, I don’t think so. I’ve never really been that kind of… I don’t know, planned about it.\n\nDoctor: When those late-night eating episodes happen, do you feel out of control while you're eating?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, kind of. It’s not like I’m thinking, “I’m gonna eat all this,” it just turns into that. Like one minute I’m opening the takeout box, and then I’m going through whatever snacks are there after midnight. And then other days I’ll realize I forgot to eat almost completely because I was running around for sales calls or driving all day. That’s the part that freaked me out, how it swings around like that.\n\nDoctor: Do these late-night eating episodes upset you a lot?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it does upset me… maybe not every single time in the moment, but afterward I’m like, what am I doing? Honestly, the thing that scared me into coming in is my eating has been all over the place—like some days I forget meals completely, and then other nights I’m tearing through takeout and whatever snacks are around after midnight. And then I’m thinking about work the next day, like if I’m gonna be dragging on calls, and it just… yeah, it bothers me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I don’t know if I’d call it “depressed” exactly, but I just don’t care about stuff like I used to. Like weekends, I used to play soccer, and I’d actually look forward to it. Or I’d cook dinner for my girlfriend, try some new recipe, that kind of thing. Sometimes we’d check out those little galleries downtown, nothing fancy, just something to do.\n\nAnd at work I used to help the newer sales guys figure things out, because I remember being totally lost when I started. Lately I just… can’t make myself care about any of it. It’s like it all sounds like too much, or pointless, and then I end up not doing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad most days?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure it’s sad most days, exactly. It’s more like… flat, I guess? Like I’ll be sitting there and know I should feel something about the weekend or plans or whatever, and I just don’t really. But “sad” sounds like crying all the time or something, and I don’t know if that’s me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble falling asleep?\n\nPatient: Yeah, falling asleep is definitely a problem most nights. I’ll lie down and my brain just keeps going over sales pitches, like what I should’ve said on a call or how I’m gonna handle some account, and then suddenly it’s way later than I thought. Or sometimes I do fall asleep, but I wake up way before my alarm feeling weirdly wired, even though I know I barely got any sleep.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot about different parts of your life?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t know. I think about stuff, yeah, like work and whatever needs to get done, but I’m not sure I’d say I worry about everything. It kind of depends.\n\nLike sometimes one thing will get stuck in my head, and then I’ll start thinking about something totally different, like how I need to get my oil changed or how my kitchen light has been flickering for, I don’t know, weeks now. But I don’t know if that’s “worrying a lot” or just… normal brain noise.\n\nDoctor: When you start worrying, is it hard to stop or control the worry?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. Sometimes I can drop it if something else grabs my attention, like if I get a call or I start doing something at work. Other times I kind of loop on it, but then I’ll realize I’m thinking about something totally different anyway.\n\nLike the other day I was thinking about this invoice thing, and then somehow I was looking up tires for my car, which wasn’t even the problem. So… hard to control? Maybe sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d call it that exactly.\n\nDoctor: Do these eating episodes usually happen after you wake up from sleep during the night?\n\nPatient: Not really like I wake up and eat, no. It’s more that my eating’s been all over the place, honestly. Some days I’ll just forget to eat meals completely, and then other nights it’s after midnight and I’m tearing through takeout and snacks like I haven’t eaten in forever. That’s actually kind of what freaked me out enough to come in.\n\nDoctor: About how long have you been feeling this flat and not caring about things?\n\nPatient: I guess… about four months? It’s hard to pin down exactly because at first I thought I was just tired or annoyed, like everybody gets like that, you know. But the not caring and feeling low, and then feeling bad about stuff… that’s been there for a while.\n\nIt got more obvious after the trouble at work. Before that I could kind of tell myself it was nothing, but after that it was harder to ignore.\n\nDoctor: What happened at work before all of this started?\n\nPatient: It was this fight with my regional manager. Not like a physical fight, just… a really bad argument. That was about a month ago, and honestly this whole thing has kind of been going on since then.\n\nI keep thinking if that hadn’t happened, maybe I’d be handling stuff better, but then I get stuck thinking about other work stuff too, like numbers and clients and who said what. But yeah, the big thing before it started was that argument with him.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

I don’t know if I’d call it traumatic exactly. It really got under my skin, and I keep replaying parts of it, like what I said and what he said… but “traumatic” sounds kind of big, you know?

Before that I could kind of tell myself it was nothing, but after that it was harder to ignore.\n\nDoctor: What happened at work before all of this started?\n\nPatient: It was this fight with my regional manager. Not like a physical fight, just… a really bad argument. That was about a month ago, and honestly this whole thing has kind of been going on since then.\n\nI keep thinking if that hadn’t happened, maybe I’d be handling stuff better, but then I get stuck thinking about other work stuff too, like numbers and clients and who said what. But yeah, the big thing before it started was that argument with him.\n\nDoctor: Did that argument with your manager feel traumatic to you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it traumatic exactly. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, some. Like I’ll be entering an order, something I’ve done a million times, and then a phone buzzes or a truck goes by outside or somebody walks past the office, and suddenly I’m not on what I was doing anymore.

Then I go back and realize I missed something stupid, like a basic detail I normally would catch. It’s annoying because it’s not even big stuff distracting me. It’s just little things pulling me off track.

Like, some days I’ll just completely forget to eat, and then other nights I’m up after midnight just tearing through takeout and snacks. I know that sounds kind of small, but it freaked me out a bit. I mean, not every time, but on those nights it feels like I start with just, like, “I’ll eat something quick,” and then suddenly I’ve gone through takeout and whatever snacks are around. It’s weird because other days I’ll just totally skip meals without meaning to. My schedule’s always been kind of all over the place with sales and being on the road, so I used to blame it on that, but lately it feels more extreme, I guess.\n\nDoctor: During those late-night eating episodes, do you feel like you can’t stop once you’ve started?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, that’s kind of what freaked me out. I’ll think I’m just going to eat something quick, and then it turns into, like, takeout plus whatever snacks are sitting around, and it’s after midnight and I’m still going. It’s weird because then other days I’ll forget to eat at all, which I know sounds dumb, but with my sales schedule I’m always running around, so I used to just blame it on that. But lately it feels more out of control, I guess.\n\nDoctor: When those late-night episodes happen, do you eat what most people would consider a very large amount of food?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. I mean, I’m not sitting there measuring it or anything, but it’ll be like a whole takeout order and then chips or whatever snacks are around after midnight. And then the weird part is the next day I might realize I barely ate at all, because I was driving around for work and just forgot. That’s kind of what scared me—how all over the place it’s gotten.\n\nDoctor: After those nights when you eat a lot, do you ever make yourself vomit?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve felt gross after, like too full and annoyed with myself, but I haven’t made myself throw up. I usually just kind of sit there and feel stupid about it, or try to sleep, and then the next day I’m distracted with work stuff again.\n\nDoctor: After those nights when you eat a lot, do you try to make up for it by exercising hard, fasting, or using laxatives?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know… I mean, I haven’t, like, tracked it that way. Sometimes the next day I might not eat much, but that could just be because I’m driving all over for work and I get busy, not because I’m trying to punish myself or something. I don’t do the whole gym thing to make up for it—honestly my schedule’s too weird for that. And laxatives, no, I don’t think so. I’ve never really been that kind of… I don’t know, planned about it.\n\nDoctor: When those late-night eating episodes happen, do you feel out of control while you're eating?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, kind of. It’s not like I’m thinking, “I’m gonna eat all this,” it just turns into that. Like one minute I’m opening the takeout box, and then I’m going through whatever snacks are there after midnight. And then other days I’ll realize I forgot to eat almost completely because I was running around for sales calls or driving all day. That’s the part that freaked me out, how it swings around like that.\n\nDoctor: Do these late-night eating episodes upset you a lot?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it does upset me… maybe not every single time in the moment, but afterward I’m like, what am I doing? Honestly, the thing that scared me into coming in is my eating has been all over the place—like some days I forget meals completely, and then other nights I’m tearing through takeout and whatever snacks are around after midnight. And then I’m thinking about work the next day, like if I’m gonna be dragging on calls, and it just… yeah, it bothers me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I don’t know if I’d call it “depressed” exactly, but I just don’t care about stuff like I used to. Like weekends, I used to play soccer, and I’d actually look forward to it. Or I’d cook dinner for my girlfriend, try some new recipe, that kind of thing. Sometimes we’d check out those little galleries downtown, nothing fancy, just something to do.\n\nAnd at work I used to help the newer sales guys figure things out, because I remember being totally lost when I started. Lately I just… can’t make myself care about any of it. It’s like it all sounds like too much, or pointless, and then I end up not doing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad most days?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure it’s sad most days, exactly. It’s more like… flat, I guess? Like I’ll be sitting there and know I should feel something about the weekend or plans or whatever, and I just don’t really. But “sad” sounds like crying all the time or something, and I don’t know if that’s me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble falling asleep?\n\nPatient: Yeah, falling asleep is definitely a problem most nights. I’ll lie down and my brain just keeps going over sales pitches, like what I should’ve said on a call or how I’m gonna handle some account, and then suddenly it’s way later than I thought. Or sometimes I do fall asleep, but I wake up way before my alarm feeling weirdly wired, even though I know I barely got any sleep.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot about different parts of your life?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t know. I think about stuff, yeah, like work and whatever needs to get done, but I’m not sure I’d say I worry about everything. It kind of depends.\n\nLike sometimes one thing will get stuck in my head, and then I’ll start thinking about something totally different, like how I need to get my oil changed or how my kitchen light has been flickering for, I don’t know, weeks now. But I don’t know if that’s “worrying a lot” or just… normal brain noise.\n\nDoctor: When you start worrying, is it hard to stop or control the worry?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. Sometimes I can drop it if something else grabs my attention, like if I get a call or I start doing something at work. Other times I kind of loop on it, but then I’ll realize I’m thinking about something totally different anyway.\n\nLike the other day I was thinking about this invoice thing, and then somehow I was looking up tires for my car, which wasn’t even the problem. So… hard to control? Maybe sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d call it that exactly.\n\nDoctor: Do these eating episodes usually happen after you wake up from sleep during the night?\n\nPatient: Not really like I wake up and eat, no. It’s more that my eating’s been all over the place, honestly. Some days I’ll just forget to eat meals completely, and then other nights it’s after midnight and I’m tearing through takeout and snacks like I haven’t eaten in forever. That’s actually kind of what freaked me out enough to come in.\n\nDoctor: About how long have you been feeling this flat and not caring about things?\n\nPatient: I guess… about four months? It’s hard to pin down exactly because at first I thought I was just tired or annoyed, like everybody gets like that, you know. But the not caring and feeling low, and then feeling bad about stuff… that’s been there for a while.\n\nIt got more obvious after the trouble at work. Before that I could kind of tell myself it was nothing, but after that it was harder to ignore.\n\nDoctor: What happened at work before all of this started?\n\nPatient: It was this fight with my regional manager. Not like a physical fight, just… a really bad argument. That was about a month ago, and honestly this whole thing has kind of been going on since then.\n\nI keep thinking if that hadn’t happened, maybe I’d be handling stuff better, but then I get stuck thinking about other work stuff too, like numbers and clients and who said what. But yeah, the big thing before it started was that argument with him.\n\nDoctor: Did that argument with your manager feel traumatic to you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it traumatic exactly. It really got under my skin, and I keep replaying parts of it, like what I said and what he said… but “traumatic” sounds kind of big, you know?\n\nI mean, I’ve had bad arguments before, like with customers who think they know everything and then they complain to your boss even though you did what you were supposed to do. This one just felt more personal, I guess. But I’m not sure what counts as traumatic.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: Yeah, some. Like I’ll be entering an order, something I’ve done a million times, and then a phone buzzes or a truck goes by outside or somebody walks past the office, and suddenly I’m not on what I was doing anymore.\n\nThen I go back and realize I missed something stupid, like a basic detail I normally would catch. It’s annoying because it’s not even big stuff distracting me. It’s just little things pulling me off track.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I haven’t really been tracking the scale, but my eating has been weird. That’s honestly one of the things that made me think I should come in.\n\nSome days I just kind of forget to eat, like I’ll look up and realize I skipped lunch and dinner, and then other nights I’m up after midnight ordering takeout and going through snacks like I haven’t eaten in days. It’s embarrassing, actually. So weight-wise, I’m not totally sure, but my appetite is definitely all over the place.\n\nDoctor: Nearly every day lately, have you been getting much less pleasure from things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… pretty much. I mean, I used to actually look forward to stuff, like playing soccer on weekends, or making dinner for my girlfriend—not like fancy chef stuff, but I’d try, you know? And there are these little galleries I used to stop into, mostly just to look around, sometimes pretend I understood the weird paintings.\n\nEven at work, I used to like helping the newer sales guys figure things out, because nobody helped me much when I started. But lately it’s like… I know I used to care about those things, but I can’t really make myself care right now. It all just feels like too much effort, or kind of pointless."
